It took me several months before I was able to say that I forgave her. In that time I was sometimes very badly behaved towards her as I would lash out, or be mean and unfair, whilst all the time we were trying to maintain an air with our young children that everything was normal. Over the last ten years I've pretty much blocked it out but I remember feeling ashamed with myself for the way I acted. It took me several months before I was able to say that I forgave her. In that time I was sometimes very badly behaved towards her as I would lash out, or be mean and unfair, whilst all the time we were trying to maintain an air with our young children that everything was normal. It was a weird time. I feel bad about some of my behaviour during that time. I acted poorly. I'm trying to remember something specifically. Over the last ten years I've pretty much blocked it out but I remember feeling ashamed with myself for the way I acted. There was a day when I think I had made her feel that everything was okay, and we were being friendly with each other, but then I got really angry and started questioning her again, and making her relive details that she was already trying to put behind her. I felt bad about it pretty quickly, as I knew that she was struggling too and that this had all been very difficult for her. I had to work through things, and the marriage guidance helped too, and we are now in a very good place and it's hard to imagine that it ever happened.</p><p><br></p><p>My main feeling about the whole thing is that I was ashamed of myself and how I acted, even though I also have to cut myself some slack as I could have reacted way more badly than I did, and that what happened to us was really hard to deal with. I'm glad that it is all okay now. I am better at dealing with stressful situations now I think, mostly as a result of everything that happened. I like to think I'm a better person. We had counselling and she also had mental health issues that had contributed towards this. She identified herself as a sex addict and attended an addiction recovery programme. It took me several months before I was able to say that I forgave her. In that time I was sometimes very badly behaved towards her as I would lash out, or be mean and unfair, whilst all the time we were trying to maintain an air with our young children that everything was normal. Over the last ten years I've pretty much blocked it out but I remember feeling ashamed with myself for the way I acted.
